# Evidence appendix

This appendix records how the evidence behind the programme was gathered and graded, and lists every source consulted, so you can audit any claim.

Across 13 domains, 633 source records were collected (some papers appear in more than one domain; 608 unique papers). The thirteenth domain, training without equipment, was added on 30 October 2026.

## Method

- **Searches:** PubMed (NCBI) for the biomedical literature, and web search for guidelines and agency documents (e.g. WHO, NICE, ACSM, AASM, EFSA, NIH ODS, Endocrine Society, ICMR-NIN). Searches were run on 28 October 2026, which is the evidence cut-off (30 October 2026 for training without equipment).
- **Hierarchy:** systematic reviews and meta-analyses of randomised trials first, then randomised trials, large prospective cohorts and guidelines or position stands. Mechanistic, animal and in-vitro studies are used only as labelled supporting material.
- **Reading:** every source listed was located and read at least as an abstract (full text where available). Authors, year, title, journal, DOI and PMID were copied from database records, never from memory. Seed references that could not be located were dropped and are listed below as "not found".
- **Contradicting evidence:** each domain search deliberately looked for null and contradicting findings; they are recorded in the dossiers and cited in the app where relevant.
- **Grading:** each source was graded for the claim it supports (high / moderate / low / very low), GRADE-style: study design, risk of bias, inconsistency, indirectness to an untrained 31-year-old man, and imprecision. In the app, statements are labelled *established*, *probable*, *uncertain* or *inference*, and decisions carry a confidence rating.
- **Audit:** after the app content was written, every citation in it was checked against the recorded findings of the cited source, and the claims were corrected where they overreached (see the README for the audit summary).
- **Sources marked ✔** below are cited somewhere in the app; the others were consulted and informed the synthesis but are not cited directly.

- [pubmed] Pelland JC[Author] AND (volume OR frequency OR dose response) — Found Pelland 2025 Sports Med volume/frequency meta-regression (PMID 41343037) and Pelland 2022 RIR-methods review (PMID 35247203).
- [pubmed] Schoenfeld BJ[Author] AND weekly resistance training volume AND meta-analysis — Found Schoenfeld 2017 J Sports Sci volume meta (PMID 27433992), Schoenfeld 2016 Sports Med frequency (27102172), Schoenfeld 2019 J Sports Sci frequency (30558493).
- [pubmed] resistance training proximity to failure meta-analysis hypertrophy — Found Refalo 2023 (36334240), Robinson 2024 (38970765), Tsartsapakis 2026 advanced-systems meta (41718208).
- [pubmed] Krieger JW single multiple sets / Ralston GW weekly set volume strength / Baz-Valle E volume hypertrophy — Found Krieger 2010 (20300012), Ralston 2017 (28755103), Ralston 2018 frequency (30076500), Baz-Valle 2022 (35291645), Baz-Valle 2021 set-counting review (30063555).
- [pubmed] Grgic J frequency strength meta-analysis / repetition failure meta-analysis — Found Grgic 2018 Sports Med frequency-strength (29470825) and Grgic 2022 JSHS failure vs non-failure (33497853). Screened out capsaicin and time-of-day papers as off-question.
- [pubmed] low-load high-load resistance training meta-analysis (Schoenfeld / Lopez) — Found Schoenfeld 2017 JSCR (28834797), Lopez 2021 MSSE network meta (33433148), Schoenfeld 2016 EJSS older load meta (25530577).
- [pubmed] inter-set rest interval duration muscle hypertrophy (meta-analysis OR systematic review OR randomized) — Found Singer 2024 Bayesian meta (39205815) and Grgic 2017 rest-hypertrophy systematic review (28641044; screened, superseded by Singer 2024, not included).
- [pubmed] repetition duration hypertrophy meta-analysis OR Iversen time-efficient OR Currier network meta-analysis — Found Currier 2023 BJSM NMA (37414459), Amdi 2025 eccentric-duration meta (40692176), Zhang 2025 superset meta (39903375), Iversen 2024 superset RCT (39072654), Radaelli 2024 older-adult volume NMA (39405023), Wu 2026 non-failure meta (42410632). Screened out BFR interval, sarcopenia and cancer-patient reviews as indirect.
- [pubmed] American College of Sports Medicine position stand progression models resistance training healthy adults — Found ACSM 2009 (19204579) and the 2026 ACSM Position Stand overview of reviews (41843416) that replaces it.
- [pubmed] Schoenfeld repetition duration / Iversen No Time to Lift / Schoenfeld longer interset rest — Found Schoenfeld 2015 Sports Med (25601394), Iversen 2021 (34125411), Schoenfeld 2016 JSCR rest RCT (26605807); screened Henselmans 2014 narrative rest review and an author reply letter (not included).
- [pubmed] Damas F muscle damage MyoPS / oedema; Skarabot J neural adaptations — Found Damas 2016 J Physiol (27219125), Damas 2016 EJAP (26280652), Damas 2018 review (29282529, not included), Skarabot 2021 (33355714), Ansdell 2020 task-specific strength RCT (32363636).
- [pubmed] drop set hypertrophy meta-analysis / Halperin RIR accuracy / Moran-Navarro recovery failure — Found Havers 2026 drop-set meta (41920484), Sodal 2023 drop-set meta (37523092; concordant, summarised within Havers entry), Halperin 2022 (34542869), Moran-Navarro 2017 (28965198).
- [pubmed] Morton RW fibre activation / Wackerhage stimuli sensors / Hubal variability — Found Morton 2019 J Physiol (31294822), Wackerhage 2019 (30335577), Hubal 2005 (15947721; PubMed record lists no DOI). A 2025 bioRxiv reanalysis of the same cohort (39868167) is a non-peer-reviewed preprint and was not used.
- [pubmed] Hammarstrom ribosome biogenesis volume / Petrella satellite cell cluster / Phillips muscle protein turnover — Found Hammarstrom 2020 J Physiol (31813190), Hammarstrom 2022 Acta Physiol ribosome accumulation (35213791; supportive, not included), Petrella 2008 (18436694), Phillips 1999 (9886957), Phillips 1997 (9252485).
- [pubmed] Barbalho M[Author] AND (volume OR resistance training) AND retraction — Retraction check: Barbalho 2019 IJSPP 'ceiling effect for training volume' (31188644), Barbalho 2019 MSSE 'upper threshold ... trained women' (30779716), Barbalho 2019 EJSS (31072272) and Barbalho 2020 JSCR (29781936) are all flagged 'Retracted Publication' in PubMed and were excluded. Baz-Valle 2022 full text states retracted studies were excluded. All included sources were checked for the PubMed 'Retracted Publication' type; none flagged.
- [pubmed] Brook MS early hypertrophy deuterium oxide mTORC1 — Found Brook 2015 FASEB J (26169934) as contradicting evidence to the oedema interpretation of early hypertrophy.
- [web] Pelland 2025 full text (Springer, Europe PMC) and SportRxiv preprint — Springer page redirects to an authentication flow; Europe PMC lists the article as subscription-only and not in PMC. The SportRxiv preprint PDF was downloaded but its text is glyph-encoded and could not be extracted. Pelland 2025 is therefore summarised from its PubMed abstract only; no numeric breakpoints from it are used.
- [pubmed] ACSM 2026 position stand full text (PMC12965823) and Baz-Valle 2022 full text (PMC8884877) — PMC website returned a CAPTCHA page (not bypassed); full texts obtained through the PubMed full-text tool and keyword-extracted. ACSM 2026: most underlying reviews studied novices; strength enhanced by >=2 sessions/wk, >=80% 1RM, full ROM, 2-3 sets/session, exercise early in session; hypertrophy by >=10 sets/muscle/wk and eccentric overload; failure, periodisation, tempo (0.5-8 s), exercise order, rest (<1 vs >1 min for strength) not influential; it cites a meta-regression showing diminishing returns beyond ~2-3 sets/exercise for strength and ~18-20 weekly sets for hypertrophy, and advises at least two sets per exercise.
- [pubmed] Lasevicius T failure low-load high-load hypertrophy; single-joint vs multi-joint elbow flexor hypertrophy untrained (Gentil, Mannarino) — Found Lasevicius 2022 JSCR (31895290), Lasevicius 2018 EJSS (29564973; 20% 1RM suboptimal even volume-matched - noted, not included), Gentil 2013 (23537028), Gentil 2015 (26446291; concordant, not included), de Franca 2015 trained (26244600; not included), Mannarino 2021 (31268995).
- [pubmed] Phillips SM mixed muscle protein synthesis and breakdown after resistance exercise; Grgic rest interval strength systematic review — Found Phillips 1997 (9252485) and Grgic 2018 rest-strength systematic review (28933024).
- [pubmed] resistance training (volume OR sets OR frequency OR rest interval) (hypertrophy OR strength) meta-analysis healthy adults, 2024-2026 — Sweep for newer syntheses that might supersede older ones. Added Ramos-Campo 2024 split vs full-body meta (38595233), Cowley 2026 advanced-methods Bayesian NMA (41951916), Swinton 2024 dose-response meta (38652410), da Silva 2025 eccentric vs concentric meta (39652733). Screened out postmenopausal-women, young-women and lifespan-women reviews (indirect population) and capsaicin reviews (off-topic). Also added Pelland 2022 RIR-methods review (35247203), found in the first search.

</details>

**Open questions:**
- Exact shape of the volume-hypertrophy curve above ~15-20 weekly sets in untrained men: Pelland 2025 numeric breakpoints are subscription-only, and its SportRxiv preprint text could not be extracted.
- Validated synergist weights for specific exercises (e.g., squats for glutes, rows for rear deltoids, presses for triceps) under fractional counting.
- Optimal RIR for unsupervised novices on free-weight lifts, and how quickly RIR estimation improves with practice.
- Whether eccentric overload or eccentric-only training adds hypertrophy with conventional exercises (ACSM 2026 vs da Silva 2025).
- Per-session volume ceiling (seen only in a SportRxiv preprint by Remmert et al., not verified or used).
- Interaction with concurrent running and endurance training, an energy deficit for fat loss, and his 04:00-12:00 sleep pattern; these affect volume tolerance and hypertrophy but belong to other domains.
- Effects of resistance training on flexibility and mobility (his concern that muscle size could hinder mobility); not reviewed in this domain.
- Prediction of individual response before training: ribosome-biogenesis and satellite-cell markers are research tools, not practical tests.

- [pubmed] range of motion resistance training hypertrophy meta-analysis (2019-) — 4 hits; Pallares 2021 ROM meta (seed) and Varovic 2025 muscle-length regional hypertrophy meta retained; Saeterbakken 2025 task-specificity meta retained for test specificity; Krause Neto 2025 gluteus SR noted.
- [pubmed] (lengthened partial OR long muscle length OR longer muscle lengths) AND hypertrophy AND training (2017-) — 19 hits screened; retained Gschneidner 2025 multi-site RCT, Wolf 2025 PeerJ, Plotkin 2026 MSSE, McMahon 2026 JSCR, Wolf 2025 SMHS longitudinal-growth review, Havers 2025, Larsen 2025 calves; narrative review Pedrosa 2026 noted.
- [pubmed] citation lookup batch for seeds (Kassiano 2023, Pedrosa 2022, Maeo 2021, Maeo 2023, Kubo 2019, Plotkin 2023, Vispute 2011, Ramirez-Campillo 2013, Alizadeh 2023, Sell 2017, Frederick 2007, Wakahara 2013) — Resolved Kassiano 2023 (PMID 37015016), Pedrosa 2022 (33977835), Maeo 2021 (33009197), Maeo 2023 (35819335; epub 2022), Kubo 2019 (31230110), Vispute 2011 (21804427), Ramirez-Campillo 2013 (23222084), Sell 2017 (29237852), Frederick and Haselton 2007 (17578932), Wakahara 2013 (23657165). Plotkin 2023 lookup returned an unrelated PeerJ paper (wrong match); Alizadeh 2023 not found by citation lookup - to search by title.
- [pubmed] Unlogged searches from the interrupted session (topics inferred from the sources retained: free weights vs machines, unilateral vs bilateral, exercise order and variation, regional hypertrophy trials, spot reduction, trunk training, resistance training and ROM, male physique perception, weight-training injury epidemiology) — These sources were located and read in the earlier session, but the individual queries were not logged before it was interrupted; identifiers in each source record were taken from tool output at the time. Wolf 2023 (IJSC) and Ramirez-Campillo 2022 (Human Movement) are not PubMed-indexed and were verified via publisher pages (web). Seeds found with corrected details: Plotkin 2023 is in Frontiers in Physiology (not PeerJ), Nunes 2021 in Eur J Sport Sci (not Sports Med), Alizadeh 2023 in Sports Medicine (not BJSM); the Chaves incline-vs-flat trial is 2020 (no 2024 paper found). No new searches were run during the 2026-10-28 synthesis because every question (Q1-Q10) already had relevant sources.

</details>

**Seed references not located (not used):** Chaves 2024 (incline vs flat bench, regional pectoral hypertrophy): no 2024 paper located; the 2020 Int J Exerc Sci RCT by Chaves et al. (PMID 32922646) was used instead.

**Open questions:**
- No longitudinal trial compares vertical vs horizontal pulling for latissimus or upper-back hypertrophy.
- No longitudinal trial compares overhead pressing with lateral raises for lateral-deltoid hypertrophy.
- Dose-response of direct abdominal training for rectus abdominis hypertrophy in healthy men is unknown.
- Whether unilateral resistance training corrects left-right strength or balance asymmetries in healthy non-athletes is untested.
- Most long-muscle-length superiority data come from small trials in untrained women or single labs; replication in untrained men over more than 12 weeks is lacking, and inter-individual variability is rarely reported.
- The optimal degree of exercise variation for adherence in true novices is untested (motivation data come from trained men).
- No male-physique perception study with South Asian or Indian raters was located; cultural generalisability of V-shape and muscularity preferences is uncertain.
- Whether very large muscle mass restricts specific joint ranges (e.g., elbow or shoulder) in natural trainees has not been studied in trials located here.
- Head-to-head trials of anti-extension, anti-rotation and flexion trunk exercises for performance or injury in healthy recreational adults are lacking.
- Whether the large upper-pectoral effect of incline pressing (one once-weekly 8-week trial, Chaves 2020) replicates, and which incline angle is optimal.
- Whether hip thrusts add gluteal growth when combined with deep squats rather than replacing them, and their effect on the hamstrings (not recorded in the located abstract).
- Whether compound lifts grow the rectus abdominis in untrained men as they did in trained men, and by how much relative to direct flexion work.
- Whether lengthened-position training adds serial sarcomeres in humans; current fascicle-length evidence relies on linear extrapolation of ultrasound images.
- Long-term (more than 12 weeks) free-weight vs machine hypertrophy comparisons in untrained men with precise estimates are lacking (pooled hypertrophy 95% CI -0.40 to 0.29).
- Energy-matched abdominal spot-reduction trials using MRI to separate subcutaneous from visceral fat are lacking; the only positive abdominal trial used DXA trunk fat in 16 men.
- Recorded findings that need full-text checks: the outcome definitions behind Pallares 2021's ES 0.88 vs 0.28, the alpha used for equivalence in Gschneidner 2025, the 9.3 cm2 vastus lateralis CSA gain in Plotkin 2026, and the 1.17 kg vs 0.7 kg trunk-fat discrepancy in Brobakken 2023.

<details><summary>Search log</summary>

- [pubmed] periodization[Title] AND (hypertrophy OR strength) AND meta-analysis; (periodized OR periodised OR periodization OR undulating) AND resistance training AND meta-analysis[Publication Type] — 11 meta-analyses screened. Kept Moesgaard 2022, Williams 2017, Polito 2021 (contradicting subgroup). Harries 2015 JSCR (17 studies/510 participants, LP = UP for strength; PMID 25268290) and Zhang 2026 Front Public Health (29 studies, LP = UP; PMID 41869632) were read and are consistent with the null but omitted for parsimony. Excluded photobiomodulation, sarcopenia, cluster-set, ball-velocity and single- vs multiple-set papers.
- [pubmed] lookup_article_by_citation for 23 seed references — All seeds resolved except LeSuer 1997 (not indexed in PubMed). Corrections: Hickmott 2022 is in Sports Medicine - Open; Ogasawara 2013 = Eur J Appl Physiol 113:975 (e-pub 2012); Nuzzo 2024 reps-at-%1RM = Sports Med 54:303 (e-pub 2023); Benito 2020 = IJERPH 17(4):1285; Bell 2023 Delphi = Sports Med Open 9:87.
- [pubmed] (autoregulated OR autoregulation OR repetitions in reserve OR rating of perceived exertion)[Title] AND resistance training; Hickmott LM[Author] — Hickmott 2022 meta-analysis verified; Hickmott 2026 JSCR pilot RCT in older adults (PMID 42297625) noted but indirect; Hickmott 2025 integrated velocity model review not used (theoretical).
- [pubmed] (predicting repetitions OR repetitions in reserve OR proximity to failure) AND accuracy AND (scoping review OR meta-analysis) — Halperin 2022 exploratory meta-analysis kept; Bastos 2024 RIR scoping review read (qualitative only, not used); Swinton 2024 dose-response meta noted (outside scope).
- [pubmed] deload OR deloading — 58 hits; kept Bell 2023 Delphi, Coleman 2024 RCT, Pancar 2026 within-subject RCT in untrained men. Rogerson 2024 survey (deload ~6.4 days every ~5.6 weeks in competitive athletes; PMID 38499934) and De Marco 2024 coach survey read but omitted (practice data, not outcomes). Many hits off-topic (engineering).
- [pubmed] (detraining OR training cessation OR training interruption) AND (muscle mass OR hypertrophy OR cross-sectional area OR strength) AND (meta-analysis OR systematic review), 2015+ — Kept Rahmati 2022 myonuclear meta-analysis and Zhang 2026 detraining meta-analysis of RCTs. Older-adult detraining meta (2022) and endurance-athlete detraining review (Barbieri 2024) screened; indirect.
- [pubmed] muscle memory AND (myonuclei OR myonuclear OR methylation) AND human, 2018+ — Kept Cumming 2024 J Physiol, Psilander 2019, Seaborne 2018. Screened Moberg 2020 MSSE (molecular memory signals), Gundersen 2026 and Sharples 2023 reviews, Serrano 2025 critical evaluation (no abstract available; not cited), Eftestol 2020 letter, Blocquiaux 2020 (older men).
- [pubmed] (time of day OR time-of-day OR diurnal OR chronotype)[Title] AND strength AND (meta-analysis OR systematic review); Facer-Childs E[Author] chronotype — Kept Grgic 2019, Knaier 2022, Bruggisser 2023, Facer-Childs 2018. Team-sport time-of-day meta (2025) and endurance time-of-day meta (Kang 2023) screened; indirect.
- [pubmed] Del Vecchio A[Author] motor unit strength training; Moritani 1979; Skarabot 2021 — Kept Del Vecchio 2019 and Moritani and deVries 1979. Skarabot 2021 review (locus of neural adaptation unresolved) and Lecce 2025 cross-education study read; omitted for parsimony.
- [pubmed] Abe T[Author] upper limit skeletal muscle mass; fat-free mass index percentiles (Schutz 2002); Kouri 1995 — Kept Kouri 1995, Abe 2018, Schutz 2002. Broad FFMI search (225 hits) was too unspecific; no contemporary drug-tested FFMI distribution found.
- [pubmed] position stand resistance training progression; Progression models in resistance training for healthy adults — Found 2026 ACSM Position Stand (Currier et al., overview of 137 systematic reviews) that updates the 2009 ACSM progression position stand; both kept.
- [pubmed] individual response / differential responders / random error resistance training; Steele J or Dankel SJ[Author] — Kept Dankel 2019 APNM (true variability analysis with time-matched controls). Steele 2023 J Sports Sci (methods for meta-analysis of variance) and Robinson 2024 N-of-1 review read; abstracts give no pooled estimates, so not cited. Dankel and Loenneke 2020 method paper read (method only).
- [pubmed] long-term strength development powerlifting longitudinal growth modelling — Kept Latella 2024 (e-pub 2023) Sports Med. Ferrari 2025 machine-learning powerlifting model screened, not used.
- [pubmed] (Epley OR Brzycki OR prediction equations) AND (one repetition maximum OR 1RM) AND (accuracy OR validity) — 21 hits; kept Reynolds 2006 and Mayhew 1995. Mayhew 2008 (women pre/post training; RTF changed substantially within individuals) and Fashi 2024 RPE model read, omitted for parsimony.
- [web] LeSuer 1997 accuracy of prediction equations 1-RM bench press squat deadlift (web) — Publisher abstract page located (journals.lww.com) but returned HTTP 402; Semantic Scholar API rate-limited. Abstract could not be read directly, so the paper is listed under notFound and not cited.
- [pubmed] Wernbom 2007; DeFreitas 2011; Damas 2016; Conceicao 2018; Ahtiainen 2003; Narici 1996 (citation lookup) — Kept DeFreitas 2011, Damas 2016, Conceicao 2018, Ahtiainen 2003. Wernbom 2007 abstract lacks rates (not used). Narici 1996 (7 men, quadriceps CSA +13 to +19% in 6 months) read but omitted in favour of the larger Ahtiainen 2016 pooled data.
- [pubmed] block periodization AND (resistance training OR strength training) AND (hypertrophy OR maximal strength OR 1RM) — 15 hits, nearly all in trained athletes. Kept Bartolomei 2023 (volume-equated mixed-session vs block, trained men). No block-periodization trial in untrained adults with hypertrophy outcome found.
- [pubmed] PMC full texts / PMC pages: Nuzzo 2024 (PMC10933212), Seaborne 2018 (PMC5789890), Benito 2020 (PMC7068252), Coleman 2024 (PMC10809978), Ahtiainen 2016 (PMC5005877) — Read to extract decision-critical numbers missing from abstracts (reps table and SDs; sample size and lean-mass changes; intervention durations and outcome-specific pooled estimates; between-group credible intervals; durations and responder thresholds).

</details>

**Seed references not located (not used):** LeSuer DA, McCormick JH, Mayhew JL, et al. 1997, Journal of Strength and Conditioning Research 11(4):211-213 (accuracy of 1-RM prediction equations for bench press, squat and deadlift): citation and publisher page located (https://journals.lww.com/nsca-jscr/abstract/1997/11000/the_accuracy_of_prediction_equations_for.1.aspx) but the abstract could not be read (HTTP 402; not indexed in PubMed), so it is not cited.; Non-seed: Serrano 2025 critical evaluation of the human muscle-memory literature was located in the muscle-memory search, but no abstract was available, so it was neither read nor cited (it may contain contradicting arguments relevant to Q4b).

**Open questions:**
- No trial has compared progression schemes (double progression vs linear load progression vs RIR autoregulation) in true novices over >=6 months, including adherence outcomes.
- Does planned variation or periodization improve adherence or enjoyment in boredom-prone recreational trainees? No included trial measured adherence.
- Whether periodization adds 1RM benefit for untrained people is unresolved (Williams 2017 training-status moderator vs Moesgaard 2022 LP = UP in untrained vs Polito 2021 opposite subgroup); the coding of the Williams 2017 moderator should be checked in the full text.
- Do planned deloads affect long-term hypertrophy, injury or adherence in novices? Current RCTs last 8-9 weeks, and the best deload frequency and structure are unknown.
- Measured 12-month and multi-year lean-mass trajectories (DXA/MRI) in drug-free novice men, including South Asian men, are lacking; year-1 projections here are inference.
- How much a deliberate energy deficit (his fat-loss goal) reduces expected novice lean-mass gains is outside this dossier and should be reconciled with the nutrition dossier.
- True inter-individual variance in hypertrophy remains contested (Dankel 2019 vs within-person data in Hammarstrom 2020); adequately powered repeated-intervention designs are needed.
- Whether retained myonuclei or epigenetic marks meaningfully accelerate human regrowth is unresolved (Cumming 2024 vs Rahmati 2022 and Psilander 2019; Seaborne 2018 uncontrolled; Serrano 2025 could not be read).
- Maintenance doses were derived from people with >=16 weeks of training; whether newly trained novices need more is untested.
- Effects of training ~1 h after a late wake time (phase-delayed sleeper) on acute performance and long-term adaptation are untested; his chronotype and circadian phase are unknown.
- RIR accuracy in the first weeks of training for complete beginners, and whether personal calibration (predicted vs achieved reps) improves it, are poorly characterized.
- 1RM prediction accuracy for free-weight squat and deadlift and for common machines (rows, pulldowns, overhead press) is not covered by readable sources (LeSuer 1997 not readable).
- VO2max retention after detraining in recently trained young men: the 2026 meta-analysis suggests VO2max stays above baseline, but data specific to novices' newly acquired gains are sparse.
- Contemporary FFMI distributions in drug-tested athletes, especially South Asian men, are lacking; the ~25 ceiling rests on a small 1995 self-report sample.
- Reliability and responsiveness of his tracking tools (consumer BIA scale, tape girths, push-up and pull-up tests) are outside this dossier, yet the responder rules depend on them.

- [pubmed] changes in CRF and mortality meta-analysis; Mendelian randomization/twin physical activity mortality; Karvinen S twins — Searched for evidence against a causal interpretation; retained Karvinen 2015 (discordant monozygotic twins, rat selection lines). Fitness-change searches returned only clinical-population reviews (excluded).
- [pubmed] Persinger talk test; Reed JL talk test; Tanaka age-predicted HRmax revisited; Gellish HRmax; talk test systematic review; RPE validity; HRmax equation accuracy — Persinger 2004, Reed & Pipe 2014, Gellish 2007 found; Tanaka 2001 resolved via citation lookup (PMID 11153730). Vieira 2022 talk-test systematic review (cardiac patients) retained as indirect support for the talk-test/VT1 correspondence. Shookster 2020 (n=99, equation agreement) screened, superseded by Nes HUNT (n=3320). Marsh 2012 (submaximal VO2max prediction) excluded.
- [pubmed] Scherr 2013 RPE (citation lookup); Chen MJ RPE meta-analysis; Nes BM HUNT HRmax — Retained Scherr 2013 (n=2560), Chen 2002 meta-analysis (validity coefficients ~0.6), Nes 2013 (SEE 10.8 bpm).
- [pubmed] manual pulse palpation accuracy during/after exercise (two query variants) — No relevant validation study located; recorded under openQuestions.
- [pubmed] Wewege/Donnelly/Willis/Jayedi/Viana author searches; energy compensation doubly labelled water — Retained Jayedi 2024 (116 RCTs), Viana 2019, Wewege 2017, Martin 2019 E-MECHANIC, Fong Yan 2018 (dance vs other exercise). Donnelly 2013 and Willis 2012 resolved via citation lookup. Silva 2018 NEAT-compensation review and Westerterp 2017 screened, not retained.
- [pubmed] Stoggl polarized; Oliveira polarized meta-analysis; Rosenblat polarized threshold; polarized[Title] meta-analysis 2022+ — Retained Silva Oliveira 2024 (title differs from seed recollection 'not superior'), Rosenblat 2025 IPD network meta-analysis, Stoggl & Sperlich 2014. Rosenblat 2019 (3 trials) and Nost 2024 (narrative SR) screened, superseded.
- [pubmed] Videbaek running injuries per 1000 h; Buist graded training novice runners; Montero haematological adaptations; Apro S6K1 AMPK; Egan & Sharples Physiol Rev; Granata mitochondrial reviews — Retained Videbaek 2015, Buist 2008 (GRONORUN RCT), Montero 2015, Apro 2015, Granata 2018 (two reviews). Egan & Sharples 2023 screened; abstract does not name the kinases, so not used as a source for specific claims.

</details>

**Seed references not located (not used):** Validation study of manual pulse palpation (radial or carotid) for exercise heart rate in healthy adults: two PubMed query variants returned no relevant study.; Meta-analysis of serial change in cardiorespiratory fitness and mortality in general (non-clinical) populations: fitness-change searches returned only clinical-population reviews, which were excluded.; Mendelian randomisation evidence on physical activity or CRF and mortality: none retained; the only evidence against a causal interpretation in this dossier is the Karvinen 2015 discordant-twin and rat study.; A human source whose abstract verifies the CaMKII and p38 MAPK arms of training-induced mitochondrial biogenesis: Egan & Sharples 2023 (Physiol Rev) was screened, but its abstract does not name these kinases, so it was not added and these steps are labelled theoretical.; Seed citation 'Wen 2019, J Sports Sci' was not found as cited; the matching paper is in J Sci Med Sport 22(8):941-947 (PMID 30733142) and is included as Wen2019_HIITprotocolsMA.; Seed recollection of the Oliveira polarised-training meta-analysis as concluding 'polarised not superior' did not match the located paper (Silva Oliveira 2024, PMID 38717713), which reports a small VO2peak advantage confined to highly trained athletes and short interventions.

**Open questions:**
- How accurate are manual pulse palpation and consumer wrist optical heart-rate devices (e.g., a Fitbit) during and immediately after interval exercise? No validation evidence is in this dossier.
- Is 4 x 4 min superior to sprint intervals or MICT for VO2max in untrained men? The 4 x 4 vs SIT RCTs are in well-trained adults from one research group with commercial affiliations, and Molmen 2024 found similar VO2max change across modes.
- Does interference with hypertrophy or lower-body strength appear in untrained men when endurance volume exceeds ~4 sessions or ~200-300 min/week for longer than 16 weeks? Trial endurance doses were modest.
- Is running or cycling the better concurrent-training partner? Meta-analyses disagree in direction (Wilson 2012 and Lundberg 2022 vs Sabag 2018; Schumann 2021 null).
- How do treadmill running, incline walking, rowing, elliptical and stair-climbing compare for VO2max gain, energy expenditure, interference and injury? No comparative source was searched for or located in this dossier.
- How much of the CRF-mortality and step-mortality gradients is causal, and does raising one's own CRF reduce risk proportionally? Karvinen 2015 suggests genetic confounding; no Mendelian randomisation or fitness-change meta-analysis was retained.
- Are step-CVD associations present in adults under 60 with longer follow-up? Paluch 2023 found none over ~6 years, probably owing to few events.
- Does zone 2 training confer any benefit over equal-time higher-intensity training for health outcomes in untrained adults over months?
- What intensity distribution is optimal for untrained novices? All distribution trials in this dossier are in athletes.
- What is the aerobic-exercise fat-loss dose-response in lean young men who track intake? Jayedi 2024 trials were in overweight or obese, mostly older adults.
- Does training and sleeping at an unusual circadian phase (wake 12:00, train 13:00, sleep ~04:00) alter adaptation to aerobic or concurrent training? Not addressed by any source here.
- What is the reliability (typical error) of a 3-km run or 20-min bike time trial in novices, and how well does it track VO2max change?
- Are unsupervised HIIT programmes as effective and safe as the supervised programmes in the Cochrane review, for a person who trains alone?
- Do martial-arts training formats give aerobic and coordination benefits comparable to dance or recreational soccer? No martial-arts source was located.
- Full-text checks needed for internally inconsistent records: Held 2026 (strength sequence SMD 1.69 with p<0.001 described as a trend); Viana 2019 (between-group fat-mass difference larger than both within-group losses); Helgerud 2007 (absolute VO2max values that do not reproduce the reported 5.5% and 7.2% gains); Schumann 2021 (hypertrophy SMD -0.01 not centred in its CI of -0.16 to 0.18).

- [pubmed] (runners[Title] OR running[Title]) AND (prevent*[Title] OR prevention[Title]) AND injur*[Title] AND (randomized OR randomised OR trial OR meta-analysis OR systematic review)[Title] — 25 hits; kept Wu 2024 Sports Med meta-analysis (null overall) and Leppanen 2024 BJSM Run RCT (novices). Noted but not cited: Toresdahl 2019/2020 Sports Health (self-directed 10-min strength programme, NYC first-time marathoners, RR 0.97, 95% CI 0.57-1.63, null), Taddei 2020 AJSM (foot-core training, positive), Naderi 2025 Res Sports Med (online multicomponent programme, IRR 0.53), SPRINT 2022 educational programme (null).
- [pubmed] (running surface OR surface stiffness OR treadmill versus overground OR concrete OR asphalt OR grass OR trail) AND (running-related injury OR running injuries OR running biomechanics) AND (systematic review OR meta-analysis) — 19 hits; kept Mitchell 2023 (surface compliance). Noted Keast 2022 Sports Med (tibial load higher with barefoot, unfamiliar minimalist shoes, longer stride, faster speed; lower on treadmill) and Viljoen 2022 living review (training on asphalt a risk factor in trail runners) - not cited separately. No prospective study isolating surface as an injury cause in road runners found.
- [pubmed] Moore IS[Author] AND (beginner runners running economy OR economical running technique review) — Moore 2016 Sports Med review (preferred stride length near-optimal for economy) noted, not cited.
- [pubmed] Impellizzeri FM[Author] AND acute chronic workload ratio conceptual issues — Impellizzeri 2020 IJSPP critique (ACWR lacks causal basis; ratio artefacts) - noted; not cited separately because the runner-specific cohort (Frandsen 2025) directly tested ACWR.
- [pubmed] Taube W[Author] AND spinal supraspinal adaptations balance training; Robling AG[Author] AND osteocyte/sclerostin review; Miller BF[Author] AND collagen synthesis tendon; Komi PV/Nicol C AND stretch-shortening cycle review; Heinemeier KM 14C — Mechanism sources retained: Taube 2008, Robling & Bonewald 2020, Miller 2005, Nicol 2006 (Komi 2000 J Biomech also read - SSC force enhancement via short-latency stretch reflex - not cited separately), Heinemeier 2013.
- [pubmed] Screening decisions after full read of abstracts — To keep the dossier focused, the following verified sources were read but not retained: Kluitenberg 2015 Sports Med (time-loss injury proportions 3.2-84.9%, high heterogeneity), Willwacher 2022 Sports Med (biomechanical risk factors conflicting to moderate), Rio 2026 Gait Posture (minimalist transition raised injury risk, 313 participants, very low certainty), Ramirez-Campillo 2021 J Sports Sci (jump training in runners, 2-5 km time trial ES 0.88), Bohm 2015 Sports Med Open (tendon stiffness SMD 0.70), Slimani 2016 J Hum Kinet (seed; narrative team-sport review), Mason 2022 Sports Med Open (male ankle-sprain risk factors incl. poorer dynamic balance).

</details>

**Seed references not located (not used):** Seed references: none unlocatable. Every seed recorded in the search log (Videbaek 2015, Nielsen 2014 JOSPT, Buist 2008, Schuster Brandt Frandsen 2025, Nigg 2015, Heiderscheit 2011, Markovic 2007, Slimani 2016, Lesinski 2015, Kummel 2016, Springer 2007, Fong Yan 2018, Lauersen 2014) was verified on PubMed; Slimani 2016 (J Hum Kinet, narrative team-sport plyometric review) was verified but not retained.; RCT comparing run-walk (walk-run) with continuous running for injury in novice runners: searched, none found.; Prevention RCT of cadence (step-rate) retraining in healthy, pain-free runners: none found; injury data exist only for runners with patellofemoral pain.; Trial comparing running shoes with non-running shoes: none exists according to the Cochrane review.; Prospective study isolating running surface as a cause of injury in road runners: none found.; Adult-specific jump-rope trials, and adverse-event or injury data from plyometric trials in untrained adults: none found (most plyometric trials did not report soreness, pain or injury).; Normative data or validated thresholds for left-right asymmetry in single-leg stance in healthy adults, and the test-retest error of individual side-to-side differences: none found.; Meta-analysis of agility-ladder or general coordination training transfer in healthy adults, and of reactive (perceptual) agility training in non-athletes: none found.; Meta-analysis of hiking or mountain hiking health effects: none found (only small RCTs and a narrative review).; Dance meta-analysis reporting cardiorespiratory or body-composition effect sizes in young adult men: none found.; Injury incidence among recreational adult beginners in martial arts: not covered by the searches run for this domain.; Meta-analysis of exercise effects on BMD restricted to men aged 18-40: none found.

**Open questions:**
- Does the >10% single-session spike threshold, derived in mostly experienced runners (mean age 45.8 y), apply to young novices and to run-walk sessions, and does capping session length actually prevent injuries (no trial)?
- Does run-walk reduce injuries relative to continuous running in novices?
- Would an unsupervised, app-delivered hip-and-core programme reproduce the supervised Run RCT effect (HR 0.66) in a man training alone, given the null pooled result for runner programmes and the apparent advantage of supervision?
- Is the Run RCT ankle-and-foot programme's acute-injury signal (HR 3.60, 1.20-10.86) real or chance?
- What are the optimal plyometric frequency (3/week favoured for reactive strength vs <=2/week for stiffness) and safe contact limits for untrained adults who also run?
- Are his low eyes-open single-leg-stance times (30 s right, 20 s left) a true deficit or a protocol artefact, and is the one-third left-right gap stable across days?
- How much does generic coordination or agility-ladder training transfer to dance, martial-arts or trail-hiking skills in untrained adults?
- What injury risks do beginner martial-arts classes carry for an adult novice, and when should sparring be introduced?
- What are the specific effects of hiking (uphill and downhill work, uneven terrain) on fitness, bone and injury in young adults?
- How much do combined resistance and jump training change BMD in healthy men aged 18-40, and does an energy deficit during fat loss blunt the response (energy availability is outside this domain)?
- Heat exposure for outdoor running at about 13:00 (very likely in India) is not addressed by this dossier; treadmill use is suggested on practical grounds only.
- How concurrent running (rising to about 10 km/week) interacts with his hypertrophy priority (interference effect) is not covered here and should come from the strength and endurance dossiers.
- Lesinski 2015 record: the recorded optimal total of '>=16-19 sessions' conflicts with 3 sessions/week for 11-12 weeks (33-36 sessions); re-reading the abstract would resolve whether this is a transcription error.
- Fredette 2022 record: the novice injury-proportion range (9.4-94.9%) exceeds the overall range (8.8-91.3%); re-reading the abstract would resolve this inconsistency.

- [pubmed] Lauersen JB[Author] AND exercise interventions to prevent sports injuries meta-analysis — Found Lauersen 2014 BJSM (24100287; included) and Lauersen 2018 BJSM strength-training meta (30131332; RR 0.338; screened, not retained to avoid overlap).
- [pubmed] warm-up AND time of day AND (morning OR diurnal) AND performance AND (body temperature OR core temperature) — 27 hits. Included Kusumoto 2021 SR, Pullinger 2019 and Edwards 2026. Screened, not retained: Mirizio 2020 Sci Rep narrative review, Pullinger 2018 (passive heating to evening rectal temperature did not remove morning repeated-sprint deficit), Robertson 2024, music and nap studies.
- [pubmed] Garber CE[Author] AND quantity and quality of exercise position stand — Found ACSM 2011 position stand (21694556).
- [pubmed] sports massage / compression garments recovery / heat therapy DOMS meta-analysis — Included Davis 2020 massage meta, Negyesi 2022 and Li 2025 compression metas (conflicting), Wiecha 2025 umbrella review. Screened, not retained: Dakic 2023 massage SR, Hou 2026 network meta, O'Riordan 2023 (compression and blood flow), Lee 2022 (compression haemodynamics), Wang 2021 Phys Ther Sport and Wang 2022 J Rehabil Med heat and cold metas (heat and cold reduce DOMS pain; covered by Wiecha 2025).
- [pubmed] Law RY[Author] AND Herbert RD[Author] AND warm-up cool-down delayed onset muscle soreness — Found Law and Herbert 2007 RCT (17535144).
- [pubmed] delayed onset muscle soreness mechanisms review nerve growth factor GDNF bradykinin — Found Mizumura and Taguchi 2024 J Physiol Sci (38267849).
- [pubmed] terminology and classification of muscle injuries in sport Munich consensus statement — Found Munich consensus (23080315). DOMS classification (type 1B) and distinguishing features verified in PMC full text via WebFetch.
- [pubmed] Nosaka K[Author] AND how long does the protective effect on eccentric exercise-induced muscle damage last — Found Nosaka 2001 MSSE (11528337).
- [pubmed] Mayorga-Vega D[Author] AND sit-and-reach AND meta-analysis — Found Mayorga-Vega 2014 JSSM (24570599; no DOI in record).
- [pubmed] reliability active knee extension straight leg raise hamstring flexibility test — Found Neto 2015 J Sport Rehabil (25364856; included). Davis 2008 JSCR concurrent validity of four hamstring tests (r 0.45-0.65; knee-extension angle recommended as reference) screened, not retained.
- [pubmed] Facer-Childs E[Author] AND chronotype AND physical performance — Found Facer-Childs 2018 Sports Med Open (30357501).
- [pubmed] general warm-up specific warm-up one repetition maximum resistance exercise performance — Found Abad 2011 JSCR (21544000; included). Rappelt 2024 Front Physiol (three crossover RCTs: post-activation protocols did not improve jumps beyond a normal warm-up) screened, not retained (power-specific).
- [pubmed] lookup_article_by_citation Magnusson J Physiol 1996 p291; Andrade RJ[Author] AND nerve AND stretching — Found Magnusson 1996 J Physiol (8951730) and Andrade 2020 J Appl Physiol RCT (32853116).
- [pubmed] time of day resistance training muscle hypertrophy strength meta-analysis morning evening — Found Grgic 2019 Chronobiol Int (30704301).
- [pubmed] retraction check — PubMed article types for every included record were inspected; none is flagged as a retracted publication or carries a retraction notice. Pinero 2024 PMC page showed no retraction notice in the portion read.

</details>

**Seed references not located (not used):** Afonso 2023 (warm-up umbrella review): no umbrella review of warm-up by Afonso et al. located in PubMed. Found instead the Afonso 2023 Sports Med commentary 'Revisiting the whys and hows of the warm-up' (PMID 37658965; opinion, not an evidence synthesis) and the 2026 IJSPP Delphi consensus on warm-up (Boullosa et al., PMID 42476526), which is used.; Ribeiro 2021 (warm-up before resistance training): only Ribeiro et al. 2020 IJERPH (PMID 32971729) was located; no 2021 Ribeiro paper on warm-up before resistance training was found.; Pinero 2024 (cold-water immersion and hypertrophy meta-analysis, Eur J Sport Sci) PMID: the record was located and read through PMC (PMC11235606; DOI 10.1002/ejsc.12074), but no PMID was displayed or retrieved, so the pmid field is left blank rather than filled from memory.

**Open questions:**
- No study in this dossier included late or shifted sleepers: does training within 1-2 h of waking (13:00 after a 12:00 wake time) impair acute performance or long-term gains, and does the time-of-day specificity of strength adaptation (Grgic2019_time_of_day_RT) follow biological rather than clock time?
- What is the minimum effective general and specific warm-up for hypertrophy-oriented sets in untrained adults, judged by repetitions completed and long-term growth rather than acute 1RM or bar velocity (Abad2011_general_specific_warmup; Ribeiro2020_specific_warmup_RT)?
- The chronic stretching dose-response is unresolved: no significant moderators in Konrad2024_chronic_stretch_ROM versus positive dose signals in Arntz2023_chronic_SS_strength, Thomas2018_stretch_dose_ROM and Nakamura2021_HI_vs_LI_SS. Head-to-head RCTs of weekly time and intensity for the hamstrings are needed.
- Does adding stretching to full-range resistance training improve ROM beyond resistance training alone in novices? The recorded data compare each against control or stretching alone (Alizadeh2023_RT_ROM).
- How much stretching maintains ROM over months? Retention data cover only 2-6 weeks of detraining (Konrad2025_detraining_ROM).
- For a toe-touch or hamstring limitation, what share is neural (sciatic nerve mechanosensitivity) versus muscular? Tissue-specific adaptation has been shown only in an ankle model (Andrade2020_muscle_nerve_stretching).
- Reliability of self-administered flexibility tests (fingertip-to-floor, knee-to-wall, phone-inclinometer straight-leg raise) in healthy adults is not covered, nor is the minimal detectable change of fingertip-to-floor in healthy people (Perret2001_fingertip_floor_test studied patients) or the reliability of the sit-and-reach test (Mayorga2014_sit_and_reach_validity covers validity only).
- The 'active straight-leg raise' named in Q9 is only partly covered: Neto2015_AKE_SLR_reliability reports a straight-leg raise without stating, in the recorded finding, whether it was active or passive.
- Do cool showers, or cold-water immersion separated from lifting by several hours, blunt hypertrophy? All CWI studies here applied immersion soon after sessions (Roberts2015_CWI_RT_adaptation; Pinero2024_CWI_hypertrophy_meta; Grgic2023_CWI_strength_meta).
- Do massage, foam rolling, compression or heat affect long-term hypertrophy or strength? No source in this dossier measured adaptation outcomes for these modalities.
- Anti-inflammatory drugs or analgesics for DOMS, and their effect on training adaptation, were outside this dossier despite the COX-2 pathway implicated in rats (Mizumura2024_DOMS_neurochemistry).
- What first-bout dose of ordinary multi-joint resistance exercise produces a protective repeated-bout effect in novices, and how long does it last? The recorded time course comes from maximal eccentric elbow-flexor exercise (Nosaka2001_RBE_duration).
- Li2025_compression_strength_meta reports negative g values as restorative effects; its full text is needed to confirm the sign convention and confidence intervals.
- Heat therapy for DOMS shows a large but class IV effect (Wiecha2025_DOMS_umbrella); whether heat affects hypertrophy is untested here.
- Systemic red flags after unaccustomed exercise (e.g., exertional rhabdomyolysis) were not covered and should be sourced in the safety domain; whether his left-right single-leg-stance difference exceeds measurement error belongs to the balance domain.

- [pubmed] spot reduction / localized fat AND exercise AND meta-analysis or systematic review (2015+); 'spot reduction'[Title] — No exercise spot-reduction meta-analysis surfaced in top hits (others were proton-therapy or device studies); found contradicting RCT Brobakken 2023.
- [pubmed] (time-restricted eating OR intermittent fasting) AND resistance training AND lean/fat-free mass AND meta-analysis (2020+) — Ali 2026 Nutr Res (8 RCTs, GRADE) selected; Verdi 2026 (5 RCTs) read and consistent (FFM -0.48 kg, 95% CI -2.52 to 1.56) but not added to sources to limit redundancy; Xie 2024 network MA read but not used (indirect comparisons).
- [pubmed] ketogenic diet AND resistance training AND lean/fat-free mass AND (meta-analysis OR systematic review) (2019+) — Ashtary-Larky 2021/2022 Crit Rev Food Sci Nutr MA selected; Vargas-Molina 2022 (5 studies, no FFM difference when surplus) and Kang 2020 SR read as context.
- [pubmed] (resting metabolic rate OR REE OR BMR)[Title] AND (Indian OR South Asian)[Title] — Song 2016 (Asian-Indian vs Chinese men) and Joseph 2017 (Indian weightlifters) selected; paediatric and T1D studies excluded.

- [web] NASEM 2023 Dietary Reference Intakes for Energy, DLW equations and PAL categories — nationalacademies.org chapter read for Table 5-5 male TEE equations and RMSE 339 kcal/d; PAL category values from search summary of Table 7-1.
- [web] Hall 2011 Lancet full text (PMC3880593) — Full-text read 1 of 2: 100 kJ/d per kg rule, half-time ~1 y, tissue energy densities.
- [web] Ross 2020 Nat Rev Endocrinol full text (PMC7027970) — Full-text read 2 of 2: BMI-specific and ethnicity-specific waist thresholds; measurement sites.
- [web] NICE NG246 waist-to-height ratio recommendations — HTTP 403; not verified; WHtR boundary taken from Browning 2010 and Ashwell 2012.
- [web] ICMR-NIN 2020 energy requirements Indian men BMR equation PAL — Search returned only secondary sources; official NIN brief_note.pdf downloaded but not machine-readable; not used.
- [pubmed] Citation lookups: Browning 2010 Nutr Res Rev; Lafontan 2009 Prog Lipid Res; Stallknecht 2007 Am J Physiol — Abstracts read.

</details>

**Seed references not located (not used):** All 26 seed references were located; corrections: Murphy and Koehler 2022 was e-published 2021; Byrne 2018 (MATADOR) e-published 2017; the Helms 2023 seed ('small vs moderate surplus') is actually maintenance vs 5% vs 15% ('Small and Large Energy Surpluses', Sports Med Open 9:102); Ashtary-Larky 2020 PubMed title reads 'gradual weight loss. rapid weight loss' (published as 'v.').; Barakat 2020 (Strength Cond J) is not indexed in PubMed; verified only through the LIDA bibliographic record and abstract summary.; Harris-Benedict 1919 original paper not located or read; its coefficients are not reproduced here (only Mifflin 1990's finding that it overestimates by ~5%).; Katch-McArdle equation original source not located; coefficients not verified (it was tested in Joseph 2017).; Cunningham 1980 (Am J Clin Nutr 33) equation not read; Cunningham 1991 (370 + 21.6 x FFM) used instead; ten Haaf 2014 abstract does not say which Cunningham version it tested.; ICMR-NIN 2020 Nutrient Requirements for Indians (energy chapter): official PDF could not be parsed; BMR method and PAL values for Indian men not verified.; NICE NG246 waist-to-height ratio recommendations: page returned HTTP 403; not verified.; Gallagher 2000 numerical %BF ranges (tables) and Misra 2009 numerical BMI cut-offs (full text) not verified; Asian Indian men's waist threshold >=90 cm verified via Ross 2020 Table 2.; No exercise spot-reduction meta-analysis was located in this session's searches.

**Open questions:**
- Validity of consumer foot-to-foot BIA (Renpho Elis 1) for tracking FFM and fat change in South Asian men was not reviewed in this domain.
- Validated REE/TEE equations for young South Asian men across training status: direction of equation bias is unresolved (Song 2016 suggests overestimation; Joseph 2017 underestimation in muscular men).
- Optimal energy surplus for hypertrophy in true novices: no RCTs located.
- Minimum sustainable body-fat percentage for men in health and hormonal terms: no outcome-based data; male LEA thresholds are extrapolated.
- Whether diet breaks benefit lean novices doing RT.
- Effect of his late sleep schedule (~04:00-12:00) and night-time desk work on energy partitioning and appetite was not reviewed here.
- Accuracy of wrist-worn wearables (e.g., Fitbit) for estimating TEE to set intake was not reviewed here.
- Magnitude of recomposition in free-living untrained men eating self-cooked Indian diets, without controlled feeding.

- [pubmed] Citation lookup of 21 seed references (Morton 2018, Nunes 2022, Tagawa 2021, Jager 2017, Helms 2014, Schoenfeld & Aragon 2018, Moore 2009, Witard 2014, Macnaughton 2016, Trommelen 2023, Snijders 2015, Schoenfeld 2013, Kerksick 2017, Messina 2018, Devries 2018, Henselmans 2022, Whittaker 2021, Reynolds 2019, Aune 2017, Hall 2019, Schoenfeld 2015) — 20 of 21 matched directly; Tagawa 2021 not matched by citation (found by author search); Macnaughton 2016 ambiguous, resolved to PMID 27511985 by abstract.
- [pubmed] Tagawa[Author] AND protein intake AND muscle mass AND dose-response — Found Tagawa 2021 Nutr Rev (PMID 33300582) and Tagawa 2022 Sports Med Open strength dose-response (PMID 36057893).
- [pubmed] (protein intake OR high-protein) AND (energy restriction OR caloric restriction OR energy deficit) AND lean mass AND (meta-analysis OR systematic review), 2017- — Hudson 2020 Adv Nutr (PMID 31794597) relevant; other hits (menopause, preterm infants, military, obesity CR, low-carb CVD) not directly relevant and not used.
- [pubmed] fasted versus fed exercise performance systematic review meta-analysis — Vieira 2025 (fasted vs fed RT, PMID 41316673) used; Kazeminasab 2025 (metabolic markers) not needed.
- [pubmed] (pre-sleep OR presleep OR bedtime) protein ingestion AND (muscle mass OR hypertrophy) AND (systematic review OR meta-analysis) — Only Dela Cruz 2021 (metabolism/appetite, not muscle) found; not used. Joy 2018 RCT located by citation as contradicting evidence.
- [pubmed] (animal protein OR plant protein) AND lean mass AND resistance training AND meta-analysis, 2019- — Candidate PMID 33670701 to verify; Messina 2018 re-found.
- [pubmed] indispensable amino acid digestibility dual isotope tracer Indian adults legumes DIAAS — Candidate PMID 31374575 to verify.
- [pubmed] Citation lookup: Hevia-Larrain 2021, Kashyap 2018, Shams-White 2017, Lane 2024, Ma 2022 NEJM, Sawka 2007, Thomas 2016, Wolfson 2016, Joy 2018, Reis 2021 — All matched except Reis 2021 (ambiguous).
- [pubmed] get_article_metadata: PMIDs 28698222 (Morton 2018 re-check), 28404575, 17277604, 31374575, 33670701 — The Morton abstract gives the 1.62 g/kg/day breakpoint without a CI. Added Shams-White 2017 (bone, Q5), Sawka 2007 ACSM fluid position stand (Q10) and Kashyap 2019 Indian legume digestibility (Q4/Q9). The Lim 2021 animal vs plant meta-analysis (PMID 33670701) was read but not added because of the 4-new-source cap (see openQuestions).
- [websearch/webfetch] ICMR-NIN Dietary Guidelines for Indians 2024 pdf nin.res.in — Located the official DGI 2024 PDFs on nin.res.in, but WebFetch failed: 404 for DGI24thJune2024fin.pdf, a DNS failure for the main.icmr.nic.in copy, and DGI_2024.pdf exceeded the 10 MB limit. A third-party resource page was checked only for links and not used as a source.
- [websearch] Dietary Guidelines for Indians 2024 Guideline 8 protein supplements (nin.res.in only) — Search-result summaries indicate that Guideline 8 advises getting good-quality protein through food combinations and avoiding protein supplements to build muscle mass. The wording and rationale could not be verified against the full text.
- [websearch/webfetch] My Plate for the Day Dietary Guidelines for Indians 2024 food groups (nin.res.in only) — ICMR-NIN My_Plate_for_the_day_J24.pdf (4 pages) was read in full: raw weights, energy, protein, fat and carbohydrate per food group for ~2000 kcal. Recorded under ICMRNIN2024_DGI.
- [none] Synthesis session: consistency check of sources against the log above — On resumption the sources array held 24 records. Records that earlier log entries describe as added (Shams-White 2017 PMID 28404575, Sawka 2007 PMID 17277604, Kashyap 2019 PMID 31374575, ICMRNIN2024_DGI) were absent, presumably lost in the interruption. Under the task rule, new searching was limited to Q9, the only question with no source (cap 3 new PubMed sources); Q5 (bone) and Q10 (hydration) are answered as inference and flagged in openQuestions.
- [pubmed] (ICMR-NIN OR 'Indian Council of Medical Research') AND protein AND (recommended dietary allowance OR requirement OR dietary guidelines) — 314 hits; 5 top hits fetched (GBD burden papers, child supplementary-nutrition and child IAAO studies); none on adult protein guidance; none used.
- [pubmed] 'dietary guidelines for Indians' (phrase) — 1 hit: Manoj Shah 2025 Indian J Med Res review comparing DGI 2024 with other national guidelines (PMID 40991224); abstract read; added as ManojShah2025_DGIcomparison (Q9).
- [pubmed] ('nutrient requirements for Indians' OR 'ICMR-NIN 2020' OR 'ICMR-NIN 2024') AND protein; (India OR Indian) AND (protein requirement OR protein quality OR protein intake) AND Kurpad AV[Author]; India adults protein '0.83 g/kg' — 167, 67 and 0 hits. Screened hits were child-nutrition and programme studies (e.g., Devi 2026 J Nutr IAAO in undernourished children, PMID 42492671); no PubMed-indexed statement of the ICMR-NIN adult protein RDA was located.
- [pubmed] Kashyap S[Author] AND digestibility AND (dual isotope OR dual stable isotope) AND Indian adults — 4 hits, all abstracts read. Added Kashyap 2019 legumes (PMID 31374575) and Kashyap 2018 egg/meat (PMID 30272112) for Q9/Q4. Kashyap 2019 J Nutr black tea + egg (PMID 31127832) and Kashyap 2021 goat milk (PMID 33677496) not added (3-source cap; see openQuestions).
- [pubmed] Swaminathan S AND Vaz M AND Kurpad AV AND protein intakes in India — Swaminathan 2012 Br J Nutr 'Protein intakes in India' (PMID 23107548) abstract read; not added (cap); summarised in openQuestions.

</details>

**Seed references not located (not used):** Full text of the ICMR-NIN Dietary Guidelines for Indians 2024: not retrievable in the earlier session (404, DNS failure, file >10 MB). The My Plate for the Day PDF was read then, but its record (ICMRNIN2024_DGI) was not saved, so no DGI quantities are cited; PubMed holds only secondary descriptions (ManojShah2025_DGIcomparison added).; No PubMed-indexed statement of the ICMR-NIN adult protein RDA was located (searches on 'nutrient requirements for Indians', ICMR-NIN 2020/2024 and '0.83 g/kg'); the 0.83 g/kg/day value is quoted from background knowledge and flagged as unverified.; No systematic review or meta-analysis of pre-sleep protein and muscle mass was found (only Dela Cruz 2021 on metabolism and appetite, not used).; No DIAAS or ileal-digestibility values for cooked Indian mixed meals (dal-rice, roti-dal, rajma-chawal) in Indian adults were located; only single legumes, egg and hen meat (Kashyap 2019, 2018) and goat milk (Kashyap 2021, not added).; No bone-outcome or hydration-in-heat source is recorded; searches were not repeated in this session because Q5 and Q10 already had sources (task rule), so those parts are answered as inference.

**Open questions:**
- Restore the records lost in the interruption: Shams-White 2017 bone meta-analysis (PMID 28404575) for Q5 and Sawka 2007 ACSM fluid-replacement position stand (PMID 17277604) for Q10; Q5 (bone) and Q10 (hydration) currently rest on inference.
- Animal vs plant protein: the Lim 2021 meta-analysis (PMID 33670701, read earlier) and Hevia-Larrain 2021 (vegan vs omnivore RT RCT) were not recorded; adding them would firm up Q4.
- Pre-sleep protein: Joy 2018 (pre-sleep vs daytime casein RCT) was located as contradicting evidence but not recorded; whether pre-sleep timing adds anything once total protein is matched remains unresolved.
- Tea with protein meals: Kashyap 2019 J Nutr (PMID 31127832; n=5) found that black tea co-ingested with an egg meal lowered true IAA digestibility by 17%; relevant to chai with meals but not added (3-source cap). Should the app advise taking tea or coffee between rather than with protein meals?
- Indian diet context: Swaminathan 2012 Br J Nutr (PMID 23107548) reports that Indian diets derive almost 60% of protein from cereals of relatively low digestibility and quality, with lysine-limited PDCAAS; not added (cap).
- Novice-specific dose-response: whether intakes above about 1.6 g/kg/day add lean mass in untrained men during their first months of RT is unresolved; Morton 2018 found larger protein effects in trained people.
- The exact wording and rationale of DGI 2024 Guideline 8 on protein supplements, the My Plate for the Day quantities and the ICMR-NIN adult protein RDA remain unverified.
- The fat content of the low-fat and high-fat arms in Whittaker 2021 is not in the abstract; the minimum-fat recommendation is therefore an inference.
- His sweat rate and sweat sodium in hot months are unknown; 3-4 pre/post-session weigh-ins would individualise the hydration plan.
- Long-term (multi-year) renal safety of >2 g/kg/day in healthy adults remains unstudied; the Devries 2018 trials were mostly short.
- Food-price figures in the budget check are approximate 2024-25 urban values (inference); his actual local prices should replace them in the app.

**Open questions:**
- @{question=What exact products and doses is he taking (elemental Mg per tablet; D3 IU and frequency, weekly versus monthly 60,000 IU; folic acid 400 ug versus 5 mg; B12 form and dose; EPA+DHA per capsule; K2 form and dose; creatine dose)?; why=Needed to apply the UL checks (Mg 350 mg, D3 4,000 IU, folic acid 1,000 ug, EPA+DHA 1 g) and to judge whether his creatine intake is already saturating.; affects=System.Object[]}
- @{question=Are any of his supplements prescribed for a documented deficiency?; why=Prescribed D3 boluses or folic acid should continue as directed.; affects=System.Object[]}
- @{question=How often does he eat oily fish, and does he eat animal foods (milk or curd, eggs, meat or fish) every day?; why=Decides whether the omega-3 capsule and the B12 tablet are needed.; affects=System.Object[]}
- @{question=Does he have any kidney, liver, heart-rhythm, blood-pressure or thyroid condition, or take PPIs, metformin, anticoagulants or CYP1A2 inhibitors?; why=Changes the creatine, caffeine, magnesium, K2 and B12 advice.; affects=System.Object[]}
- @{question=How well does he sleep from 04:00 to 12:00 now (time to fall asleep, awakenings, light and noise), and when in the day does he take his current 100 mg of caffeine?; why=Sets the caffeine cut-off and whether sleep measures or melatonin need attention.; affects=System.Object[]}
- @{question=Is supplement spending part of the INR 20,000/month food budget?; why=Determines whether protein powder and optional items compete with food.; affects=System.Object[]}

- @{question=Does the Desai2024 abstract really give '7 g/d (or 0.3 g/kg/d)'?; why=0.3 g/kg/d is a loading-sized dose (23 g/d at 77 kg), so the source record may contain a transcription error.; affects=System.Object[]}

**Seed references not located (not used):** Any trial comparing keeping vs advancing a stable, late, work-constrained sleep schedule in non-shift workers.; A trial of eye masks or earplugs for daytime sleep in healthy adults (an author search for a healthy-adult eye-mask trial returned nothing; only an ICU-dominated review was found).; Any study of resistance-training performance or hypertrophy as a function of time since waking.; A systematic review or meta-analysis of the meal-to-bedtime interval with objective sleep measures in healthy adults (only cross-sectional studies found).; PSG validation of current Google Fitbit models, or of any consumer device during daytime sleep.; An evidence-based bedroom temperature target (none stated in retrieved abstracts).; Confidence intervals for the pooled effects in Gardiner 2023, Stutz 2019 and Kredlow 2015 (not in abstracts; full texts not read per protocol).; Sample sizes for Facer-Childs 2019, Mason 2022, Jewett 1999 and Iao 2021 (not stated in retrieved abstracts).; Evidence on the sleep-related supplements in his stack (magnesium glycinate, L-theanine); not searched in this domain.

**Open questions:**
- Is his late timing biological (late chronotype) or purely work-driven? A chronotype questionnaire or his free-day sleep timing would help.
- How dark, quiet and cool is his bedroom between 04:00 and 12:00, and when is sunrise at his location and season?
- When does he take his roughly 100 mg of caffeine relative to his 04:00 bedtime?
- When and how large is his last meal relative to bedtime?
- Do the Brown 2022 light thresholds transfer to people with regular but late schedules?
- Would extending sleep to 8-9 h benefit a non-athlete who already sleeps 7-8 h?
- Does training 1 h vs 2-3 h after waking change his strength performance? He can test this himself through logged loads.
- Is his gym and workplace lighting near 250 lux melanopic EDI at eye level?

<details><summary>Search log</summary>

- [pubmed search_articles] Renpho — 2 hits; 7946465 is a 1994 wastewater-microbiology review ('Renpho sludge'), irrelevant. No body-composition record.
- [pubmed search_articles] Renpho AND (scale OR impedance OR "body composition") — 1 hit (7946465, irrelevant). No Renpho validation study indexed in PubMed.
- [pubmed search_articles] (smart scale OR foot-to-foot OR leg-to-leg) bioelectrical impedance DXA body fat validity — 30 hits; screened 10 abstracts; kept Nana 2022, Nickerson 2019; excluded paediatric, anorexia, oncology and elderly-sarcopenia samples as indirect.
- [pubmed search_articles] Bioelectrical impedance analysis part I: review of principles and methods[Title] — Kyle 2004 ESPEN review.
- [pubmed search_articles] tracking changes body composition BIA DXA pre-assessment standardisation — Tinsley 2021; Siedler 2022 found via the smart-scale query.
- [pubmed search_articles] leg-to-leg pressure contact footpad electrodes (Nunez 1997 title) — Original foot-to-foot BIA validation.
- [pubmed search_articles] Impact of food and fluid intake ... body composition ... athletes (Kerr 2017 title) — Meal/fluid effects on impedance-based estimates.
- [pubmed search_articles] Relative fat mass; Woolcott OO[Author] AND Bergman RN[Author]; RFM external validation DXA — Woolcott 2018 derivation; Guzman-Leon 2019 external validation. Paediatric and epidemiological RFM papers excluded.
- [pubmed search_articles] (Navy OR Department of Defense OR Hodgdon) circumference body fat equation DXA — 2 hits: Friedl 1997, McClung 2025. Original Hodgdon & Beckett 1984 is a Naval Health Research Center report, not indexed.
- [pubmed search_articles] Deurenberg 1991 title; FFMI steroid users (Kouri 1995); FFMI percentiles (Schutz 2002) — All located.
- [pubmed search_articles] Ross R[Author] waist circumference protocol; Ashwell M[Author] WHtR meta-analysis — Ross 2008 systematic review; Ashwell 2012 meta-analysis.
- [pubmed search_articles] Estimation of VO2max from a one-mile track walk (Kline 1987 title) — Located.
- [pubmed search_articles] Consensus statement ... Asian Indians (Misra 2009 title); waist cutoff points and action levels for Asian Indians; Appropriate BMI for Asian populations — Misra 2009 consensus abstract gives no numeric cut-offs (read, not used); Misra 2006 and WHO 2004 used.
- [WebSearch] NICE guideline waist-to-height ratio 0.5 South Asian BMI thresholds (nice.org.uk); IDF South Asian waist 90 cm (idf.org, who.int) — Search summaries only. WebFetch of NICE NG246 recommendations page, NG246 PDF, NICE waist news page and the WHO 2008 waist report all returned HTTP 403.
- [pubmed search_articles] (NICE OR National Institute for Health and Care Excellence) AND waist-to-height ratio AND (guideline OR guidance), 2022 onward — Gray 2024 (corroborates NICE recommendation) and Nevill 2025 (critique) used.
- [pubmed search_articles] self-weighing systematic review meta-analysis; psychological impact of self-weighing meta-analysis; body weight fluctuation weekly pattern weekend — Madigan 2015, Benn 2016, Maher 2023.
- [pubmed search_articles] progress photographs/photos/pictures; visual estimation percent body fat photographs — No relevant study; phrase searches were mapped to 'disease progression'.
- [pubmed search_articles + get_article_metadata] Mayorga-Vega 2016 title; nonexercise prediction model HUNT — Nes 2011 title words did not match (V.O2 indexing); record confirmed by direct PMID metadata lookup; Nes 2014 found in the HUNT search.
- [pubmed search_articles] push-up exercise capacity cardiovascular events men; Normative values for the unipedal stance test (title) — Yang 2019; Springer 2007.
- [pubmed search_articles] standing long jump test-retest reliability adults; (standing broad jump[Title] OR standing long jump[Title] OR horizontal jump[Title]) AND reliability AND adults — First query hits were youth/disability/sport-specific (screened 2, excluded); second gave Marin-Jimenez 2024 (used); soccer and cerebral-palsy papers excluded.
- [pubmed search_articles] pull-up test norms adults men — Lester 2014 (reliability; norms not in abstract) and Negrete 2010 (MDC). No civilian max-rep pull-up norms found.
- [pubmed search_articles] Aune 2017 RHR dose-response meta-analysis (title); Fuller 2020 wearables systematic review (title) — Both located.
- [pubmed search_articles] Case MA[Author] AND smartphone AND physical activity AND JAMA[Journal]; smartphone pedometer step counting accuracy — Case 2015 has no abstract in PubMed (not used); Caputo 2021 used as very-low-grade evidence.
- [pubmed search_articles] Gillinov S[Author] AND heart rate monitors; Investigating sources of inaccuracy in wearable optical HR sensors (title); Fitbit sleep staging PSG meta-analysis — Gillinov 2017, Bent 2020, Haghayegh 2019.
- [pubmed search_articles] How well do activity monitors estimate energy expenditure? systematic review meta-analysis — Checked 30977740, which was Brickwood 2019 (tracker behaviour-change meta-analysis, used); other two hits not checked.
- [pubmed search_articles] Hydration of fat-free body mass (title); Bioelectrical phase angle and impedance vector analysis (title) — Wang 1999; Norman 2012 for mechanisms.
- [WebSearch + WebFetch] Google Fitbit Air tracker sensors specifications; Fitbit Air sensors (support.google.com, blog.google, store.google.com) — Device exists. Store spec page script-rendered (no text via WebFetch); Google Health Help Center 'Get started with Google Fitbit Air' fetched and used.
- [pubmed search_articles] Fitbit[Title] AND Air[Title] — 0 hits: no validation study.

</details>

**Seed references not located (not used):** No peer-reviewed validation of any Renpho scale (including Elis 1) in PubMed (searched 2026-10-28).; Original Hodgdon & Beckett 1984 Navy equation report is not PubMed-indexed; validation taken from Friedl 1997 and McClung 2025.; No study evaluating self-taken progress photographs (visual comparison) as a body-composition or adherence measure.; No source read on the reliability (technical error) of self-measured waist circumference.; Springer 2007 numeric norms for adults 18-39 y are in the full text only (not read under the abstract-only rule).; NICE NG246 and the WHO 2008 waist report could not be fetched (HTTP 403); IDF South Asian 90 cm cut-off seen only in search summaries, so it is not cited as a source.; Case 2015 (JAMA research letter, PMID 25668268) located but PubMed has no abstract; not read and not used.; No independent validation of the Google Fitbit Air in PubMed (title search, 2026-10-28).; No civilian adult pull-up norms in the abstracts located.; Google Store Fitbit Air spec page is script-rendered (WebFetch returned 38 characters); sensor list taken from search summaries only.; Jensen 2013 (Copenhagen Male Study) not retrieved; Aune 2017 meta-analysis used instead.; No meta-analysis quantifying wearable energy-expenditure error (percentage) was read; the targeted search returned Brickwood 2019 instead, so EE inaccuracy rests on Fuller 2020's qualitative conclusion.

**Open questions:**
- Which frequency and equations does the Renpho Elis 1 use, and how large is its error for change against DXA or a 4C model?
- Do the RFM, Navy and Deurenberg equations hold for South Asian men against DXA?
- Within-person reliability of the Rockport walk and 2.4 km run in hot, humid conditions.
- Exact NICE NG246 wording (WHtR bands, BMI < 35 limit, applicability to muscular adults) needs a direct check when nice.org.uk is reachable.
- Accuracy of the Google Fitbit Air for heart rate, sleep and steps.
- Reliability of self-measured waist circumference, and numeric unipedal-stance norms for men aged 18-39 (Springer 2007 full text).
- Magnitude of creatine-related changes in body water, weight and BIA outputs.

- [PubMed search_articles] (video feedback OR visual feedback) AND (squat OR deadlift OR resistance exercise technique OR lifting technique) AND (novice OR untrained OR beginners) — 9 hits; 37916599 Shin 2023 RCT used; 33501059 (VR mirror), 30357834 (chaining), 28606773 (EMG feedback) read, not used
- [PubMed search_articles] Schoenfeld BJ[Author] AND attentional focus AND (hypertrophy OR muscle thickness) — 29533715 used as contradicting evidence
- [PubMed search_articles] (exercise OR physical activity OR resistance training) AND body image[Title] AND meta-analysis[Publication Type] — 11 hits; 39912824 and 39297996 used; 38599266, 37526698, 36982136, 34245459 read, off-target
- [PubMed search_articles] (Campbell A[Author] AND Hausenblas HA[Author]) OR (exercise interventions[Title] AND body image[Title] AND meta-analysis) — 19687115 Campbell 2009 used; 23232051 (media exposure) read, not used
- [PubMed search_articles] muscle dysmorphia[Title] AND (systematic review OR meta-analysis OR review) — 14 hits; 41009236 and 40836642 used; 41749983, 35926364, 35675995, 33568203 read, not used
- [WebFetch] https://www.heart.org/en/health-topics/heart-attack/warning-signs-of-a-heart-attack — HTTP 403
- [WebFetch] https://www.cdc.gov/heart-disease/about/heart-attack.html — CDC heart-attack symptom page used
- [PowerShell] pypdf / pdftotext availability check — Neither available; PAR-Q+ PDF text not extracted

</details>

**Seed references not located (not used):** Lally et al. 2010 (habit formation, Eur J Soc Psychol) is not indexed in PubMed; abstract not verifiable with permitted tools (alternative being sought).; Numeric pain thresholds of the Silbernagel pain-monitoring model: not given in any abstract read (17307888, 21084657, 33632313, 30090678, 42316329).; Official PAR-Q+ 2025 question wording: PDF text not extractable in this session; logic taken from the official site.; ACSM 2015 algorithm branch wording (e.g., for inactive asymptomatic adults): not in the abstract.; No systematic review or meta-analysis of video self-feedback for resistance-exercise technique found in PubMed; one small RCT used.; No trial testing a calibration routine for RIR/RPE accuracy in novices was found.; No prospective injury-rate data for recreational novice lifters were found; competitive-athlete and ED data used.; No outcome data comparing spotters, safety arms or machines for injury prevention were found.; AHA heart-attack warning-signs page returned HTTP 403; CDC page used instead.; Pooled body-image effect size in Campbell 2009 is not reported in the abstract.

**Open questions:**
- Verify the numeric limits of the Silbernagel pain-monitoring model from the original figure before the app shows numbers.
- Verify the official PAR-Q+ 2025 question wording and the 12-month validity statement from the official PDF.
- Confirm India-specific emergency numbers for the red-flag card.
- Is the user's gym air-conditioned, and will any cardio be done outdoors at midday?
- User's resting blood pressure is unknown.
- Does in-app feedback on RIR prediction error improve a novice's accuracy over 8-12 weeks?
- Does unguided video self-review (without a coach choosing corrections) improve novice technique?
- Is the internal-focus hypertrophy benefit replicable beyond one small trial?
- Which brief, validated muscle-dysmorphia screener is suitable for in-app use?

## Training without equipment (Away mode)

91 sources · searched 2026-10-30 · dossier status: partial (the written syntheses were cut off by a usage limit; every recorded source was located, read and identifier-checked) · 83 cited in the app

Six PubMed searches ran in parallel: bodyweight and low-load resistance training; bands, improvised loads and isometrics; lower body without equipment (unilateral work, Nordic curls, eccentric training and flexibility); upper body without equipment (push-up variants, inverted rows); aerobic training without equipment (bodyweight intervals, stairs, exercise snacks); and short breaks (detraining, retraining, step reduction). Records are graded as elsewhere in this appendix. The app content built on them was audited claim by claim against these records.

**Not completed or not located:** the check of Hevy exercise names against the Hevy library did not run (names in the app are the most likely Hevy-style names; the logging page says to create a custom exercise if one is missing); the upper-body questions on training the lats, biceps and lateral deltoids without a bar or band, and on chair-dip shoulder loading, found no usable trials before the cut-off (the app labels that advice as inference); the classic reduced-training studies of VO₂max (Hickson et al.) were not reached, so VO₂max maintenance rests on Spiering 2021 and the short-detraining review.

**Open questions:**
- No trial has compared a complete bodyweight programme with gym training for hypertrophy in young men; the evidence is exercise-level (push-ups, suspension, bands, low loads).
- How much heavy-lift 1RM is lost over 2–4 weeks of bodyweight-only training, and how quickly it returns (only inference from load-specificity data).
- Pike push-ups, bodyweight triceps extensions, sliding leg curls and reverse crunches have no training studies; their use rests on biomechanics and practice.
- Whether bodyweight interval training maintains a VO₂max previously built with 4 × 4 intervals over weeks of travel.
